# SHED 2025 Variable Reference

Team reference for interpreting every entry in the public use codebook

Coverage: 459 entries in source order, comprising 458 named entries and one imputation flag naming template. Each record preserves the variable name, description, available value labels, measurement type, units, respondent scope, missing information, and special coding. Use Word search to find an exact variable name. Source PDF page numbers appear in each record.

# How to read this reference

Descriptions and printed value mappings come from SHED_2025codebook.pdf. Measurement types are analytical interpretations, not source storage types. Units inferred from wording are identified where appropriate. The codebook field called Units describes numeric precision, such as 1 or .01, rather than a substantive unit like dollars; this reference supplies the latter when identifiable.

Who was asked is reported only to the extent supported by this codebook. A complete response count does not establish that a question was asked of everyone. When routing is not documented, consult the supplemental questionnaire before defining the analysis denominator. Missing counts are descriptive counts in the codebook and are not weighted estimates.

A dot means numeric system missing; an empty string means string missing. Reasons for system missing are generally not separated in the entries. Special responses such as Refused, Not asked, and Don't know are listed with their actual codes and should be handled explicitly. In particular, -2 has different meanings across variables. Never turn all missing values into No or zero.

# Important documentation gaps

I40, ppstaten, ppcm0160, and ind1 provide examples rather than complete value dictionaries. The entries below reproduce only those documented examples. GH14 includes -1 without a printed meaning. The malefemale description conflicts with its displayed year coding. pphhsize5 does not establish what its highest value means. Confirm these details in the actual dataset or corrected documentation before analysis. No unavailable labels or routing rules have been invented.

# Weights and profile variables

Use weight for single year cross sectional statistics for all respondents, and weight_pop for population totals. Use panel_weight or panel_weight_pop for respondents participating in both 2024 and 2025. The codebook says population weights scale to the adult population using the March 2025 CPS. Weight selection alone does not specify a complete variance estimation method.

Many pp variables were collected by Ipsos before SHED. The codebook notes that the published report uses SHED answers rather than similar profile answers when available. Do not assume a profile response describes the respondent at the exact time of the SHED interview.

Source: Board of Governors of the Federal Reserve System, Codebook for 2025 Survey of Household Economics and Decisionmaking, supplied 119 page PDF. Dataset citation supplied in the codebook: Board of Governors of the Federal Reserve System Survey of Household Economics and Decisionmaking \[dataset\] (Washington: Board of Governors, 2026), <https://doi.org/10.17016/datasets.002>. This reference summarizes the supplied codebook only; it does not validate the underlying data or consult the separate questionnaire.

The source sometimes supplies only shortened question labels; these are preserved rather than expanded into invented question wording.
